```
+def cmd_plan_done(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
+    """Mark findings as fixed from plan context."""
+    from desloppify import state as state_mod
+    from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.state import state_path
+    from desloppify.app.commands.resolve.selection import (
+        _show_attestation_requirement,
+        _validate_attestation,
+    )
+    from desloppify.engine.plan import purge_ids
+    from desloppify.engine.work_queue import ATTEST_EXAMPLE
+
+    attestation: str | None = getattr(args, "attest", None)
+    patterns: list[str] = getattr(args, "patterns", [])
+
+    if not _validate_attestation(attestation):
+        _show_attestation_requirement("Done", attestation, ATTEST_EXAMPLE)
+        return
+
+    state_file = state_path(args)
+    state_data = state_mod.load_state(state_file)
+
+    resolved: list[str] = []
+    for pattern in patterns:
+        resolved.extend(
+            state_mod.resolve_findings(state_data, pattern, "fixed", attestation=attestation)
+        )
+
+    if not resolved:
+        print(colorize("  No open findings matching: " + " ".join(patterns), "yellow"))
+        return
+
--
+    "cmd_plan_done",
+    "cmd_plan_focus",
+    "cmd_plan_reopen",
+    "cmd_plan_skip",
+    "cmd_plan_undefer",
+    "cmd_plan_unskip",
+    "cmd_plan_wontfix",
+]
```

```
1→"""Pattern → finding-ID resolution for plan commands."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from desloppify.engine._plan.schema import PlanModel
     6→from desloppify.engine._state.resolution import match_findings
     7→from desloppify.engine._state.schema import StateModel
     8→
     9→
    10→def resolve_ids_from_patterns(
    11→    state: StateModel,
    12→    patterns: list[str],
    13→    *,
    14→    plan: PlanModel | None = None,
    15→    status_filter: str = "open",
    16→) -> list[str]:
    17→    """Resolve one or more patterns to a deduplicated list of finding IDs.
    18→
    19→    When *plan* is provided, literal IDs that exist only in the plan
    20→    (e.g. ``subjective::*`` synthetic items) are included even if they
    21→    have no corresponding entry in ``state["findings"]``.
    22→    """
    23→    seen: set[str] = set()
    24→    result: list[str] = []
    25→
    26→    # Collect all plan IDs for literal-match fallback
    27→    plan_ids: set[str] = set()
    28→    if plan is not None:
    29→        plan_ids.update(plan.get("queue_order", []))
    30→        plan_ids.update(plan.get("skipped", {}).keys())
    31→        for cluster in plan.get("clusters", {}).values():
    32→            plan_ids.update(cluster.get("finding_ids", []))
    33→
    34→    for pattern in patterns:
    35→        matches = match_findings(state, pattern, status_filter=status_filter)
    36→        if matches:
    37→            for finding in matches:
    38→                fid = finding["id"]
    39→                if fid not in seen:
    40→                    seen.add(fid)
    41→                    result.append(fid)
    42→        elif pattern in plan_ids and pattern not in seen:
    43→            # Literal plan ID (e.g. subjective::foo) not in state findings
    44→            seen.add(pattern)
    45→            result.append(pattern)
    46→    return result
    47→
    48→
    49→__all__ = ["resolve_ids_from_patterns"]
    50→
```

```
108→def resolve_lang(args) -> LangConfig | None:
   109→    """Resolve language config from args, with auto-detection fallback."""
   110→    lang_name = getattr(args, "lang", None)
   111→    if lang_name is None:
   112→        lang_name = auto_detect_lang_name(args)
   113→    if lang_name is None:
   114→        return None
   115→    try:
   116→        return lang_api.get_lang(lang_name)
   117→    except ValueError as exc:
   118→        langs = lang_api.available_langs()
   119→        langs_str = ", ".join(langs) if langs else "registered language plugins"
   120→        raise LangResolutionError(
   121→            f"{exc}\n  Hint: use --lang to select manually (available: {langs_str})"
   122→        ) from exc
   123→
   124→
   125→def resolve_lang_settings(config: dict, lang: LangConfig) -> dict[str, object]:
   126→    """Resolve persisted per-language settings from config.languages.<lang>."""
   127→    if not isinstance(config, dict):
   128→        return lang.normalize_settings({})
   129→    languages = config.get("languages", {})
   130→    if not isinstance(languages, dict):
   131→        return lang.normalize_settings({})
   132→    raw = languages.get(lang.name, {})
   133→    return lang.normalize_settings(raw if isinstance(raw, dict) else {})
   134→
```

## Analysis Report: Plan Skip/Reopen Handlers vs. Resolve Command

I've thoroughly analyzed the current implementation of `cmd_plan_skip`, `cmd_plan_reopen`, and the full `cmd_resolve` flow. Here's the detailed breakdown:

---

### 1. **`cmd_plan_skip` (override_handlers.py, lines 72-157)**

**What it does:**
- **Temporary skip** (default): Marks items as skipped in the plan only
- **Permanent skip** (`--permanent`): Calls `state_mod.resolve_findings()` with status="wontfix", then marks as skipped in plan
- **False positive** (`--false_positive`): Calls `state_mod.resolve_findings()` with status="false_positive", then marks as skipped in plan

**State changes (for permanent/false_positive):**
- Writes to state file via `state_mod.save_state()` at line 138
- Updates `findings[fid]["status"]` to "wontfix" or "false_positive"
- Logs attestation to `attestation_log` list
- Stores resolution note

**Plan changes:**
- Calls `skip_items()` (line 141) which adds items to plan's `skipped` dict
- Supports `review_after` parameter to mark for auto-resurface after N scans
- Stores skip reason, note, and attestation in plan data structure

**Output (lines 154-156):**
- Shows count: `"Skipped {count} item(s)."`
- Shows review_after hint if present

**MISSING vs. resolve:**
1. No score update display (no `print_score_update` or `_print_score_movement`)
2. No milestone celebration 
3. No next-step guidance (`_print_next_command`)
4. No query.json update
5. No narrative computation
6. No plan purge (items stay in queue while marked skipped)

---

### 2. **`cmd_plan_reopen` (override_handlers.py, lines 198-239)**

**What it does:**
- Calls `state_mod.resolve_findings(state_data, pattern, "open")` for each pattern (lines 210-212)
- Removes items from plan's `skipped` dict (lines 222-228)
- Adds reopened items back to `queue_order` if not already present (lines 229-232)

**State changes:**
- Writes to state file via `state_mod.save_state()` at line 218
- Updates `findings[fid]["status"]` to "open"

**Plan changes:**
- Removes from `plan["skipped"]`
- Appends to `plan["queue_order"]` if not already there

**Output (lines 236-238):**
- Shows count: `"Reopened {len(reopened)} finding(s)."`
- Shows plan update hint if items moved back to queue

**MISSING vs. resolve:**
1. No score update display
2. No narrative/milestone
3. No next-step guidance
4. No query.json update

---

### 3. **`cmd_resolve` (resolve/cmd.py, lines 58-155) — The GOLD STANDARD**

**Args Namespace requires:**
- `args.status`: "open" | "fixed" | "wontfix" | "false_positive" (line 61, 271)
- `args.patterns`: list[str] of patterns to match (line 274-278)
- `args.note`: optional string, **required for wontfix** (line 81-85, parsed at 280)
- `args.attest`: optional attestation string (line 283-290)
- `args.lang`: optional language override (used by resolve_lang)
- `args.state`: optional state file path (line 298)

**Pre-flight validation:**
- `_validate_resolve_inputs()` at line 61: checks wontfix requires note, non-open requires attestation
- `_enforce_batch_wontfix_confirmation()` at line 65-70: prevents large wontfix batches without confirmation

**State changes (line 78):**
- Calls `_resolve_all_patterns(state, args, attestation)` which:
  - Expands cluster names to member IDs (apply.py, line 38-50)
  - Calls `state_mod.resolve_findings(state, fid, status, note, attestation)` for each finding
  - Returns list of resolved IDs

**Plan changes (lines 92-102):**
- Best-effort: tries to call `purge_ids(plan, all_resolved)` to remove resolved items from queue
- Emits warning to stderr if plan update fails (doesn't exit)

**Score reporting (lines 114-122):**
- Captures previous score snapshot at line 71 via `_previous_score_snapshot()` 
- Calls `_print_score_movement()` at line 114:
  - Shows current quartet: overall, objective, strict, verified
  - Shows deltas for each
  - Shows wontfix debt warning if applicable
  - Shows review/fixed notation if relevant

**Narrative & milestone (lines 130-137):**
- Resolves language config via `resolve_lang(args)`
- Calls `narrative_mod.compute_narrative(state, context=NarrativeContext(...))`
- Displays milestone if present: `→ [milestone text]`

**Next-step guidance (line 139):**
- Calls `_print_next_command(state)` which:
  - Counts remaining open findings
  - Prints `"{N} finding(s) remaining — run desloppify next"`
  - Returns "desloppify next" or "desloppify scan" string

**Query.json update (lines 140-154):**
- Calls `_write_resolve_query_entry(ResolveQueryContext(...))`
- ResolveQueryContext (selection.py, lines 24-35) bundles:
  - `patterns`, `status`, `resolved` list
  - `next_command` string
  - Previous + current scores (overall, objective, strict, verified)
  - `attestation` string
  - Full `narrative` dict
  - Current `state` dict

**_write_resolve_query_entry** (apply.py, lines 71-92):
- Writes to query.json with structure:
  - `command`: "resolve"
  - `patterns`, `status`, `resolved`, `count`
  - `next_command` string
  - All score quartet + deltas
  - `attestation`, `narrative`

### 4. **`print_score_update` (score_update.py, lines 11-61)**

This is simpler than `_print_score_movement` and designed for commands that don't need detailed delta reporting.

**Signature:**
```python
def print_score_update(
    state: dict,
    prev: state_mod.ScoreSnapshot,
    *,
    config: dict | None = None,
    label: str = "Scores",
) -> None:
```

**What it does (lines 26-47):**
- Takes state and previous score snapshot
- Computes new snapshot via `state_mod.score_snapshot(state)`
- Shows quartet with deltas via `format_delta()`
- Shows strict target progress if config available (lines 49-61)

**Usage context:**
- Used by `cmd_ignore_pattern` (resolve/cmd.py, line 192)
- Could be called by plan handlers for minimal score reporting

**Can be easily called?** 
- YES, it only needs `state` and `prev` snapshot
- Doesn't require narrative or language config

### 7. **Query.py helpers — How to write query.json**

**Simple function:**
```python
def write_query(data: dict) -> OutputResult:
    """Write structured query output using default best-effort policy."""
    return write_query_best_effort(data, context="query payload update")
```

**Usage (resolve/cmd.py, line 140):**
```python
_write_resolve_query_entry(ResolveQueryContext(...))
```

Which then calls `write_query()` in apply.py.

**For plan handlers:**
- Import `from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.query import write_query_best_effort`
- Call with custom context string
- It's best-effort (prints warning to stderr if fails, doesn't exit)

### 8. **What `plan done` needs — Delegating to resolve**

From git history (commit e5712e3), the old `cmd_plan_done`:
- Took `args.attest` and `args.patterns`
- Validated attestation (same validation as skip)
- Called `state_mod.resolve_findings(state_data, pattern, "fixed", attestation=attestation)`
- Removed from plan skipped dict if present
- Did NOT show scores or query.json

**To make it delegate to resolve:**
1. Create new Namespace with:
   - `status="fixed"`
   - `patterns=args.patterns` (from plan done args)
   - `attest=args.attest` (from plan done args)
   - `note=""` (not required for fixed status)
   - `lang=None` (let resolve_lang auto-detect)
   - `state=None` (let state_path auto-detect)
   - `confirm_batch_wontfix=False` (not relevant for fixed)

2. Call `cmd_resolve(constructed_namespace)`

This gives plan done the full resolve UX (scores, milestone, next-step, query.json).

|  | ignore: `pattern --attest` |

| **Rich UX in ignore** | Score delta only (simpler) |
| **Attestation required** | fixed, wontfix, false_positive, ignore (NOT "open" or reopen) |
| **Notes required** | wontfix mandates --note |
| **Batch safety** | wontfix batches >10 require --confirm-batch-wontfix |
| **Cluster support** | resolve can expand cluster names to member IDs |
| **Rendering functions** | 6 functions in render.py (only used by cmd_resolve, not plan) |
| **Selection/validation** | 7 functions in selection.py (shared with plan commands) |
| **Apply logic** | Delegates to state_mod.resolve_findings() + cluster expansion |
| **Query.json** | Rich context entry written with full narrative/scores/next-command |
| **Tests** | Comprehensive validation of inputs, errors, and outputs |

## KEY FINDINGS FOR MIGRATION

**What plan commands are MISSING from resolve functionality:**
1. **Score movement display** – `_print_score_movement()` shows rich before/after analysis (not in plan commands)
2. **Narrative milestones** – resolve computes narrative with context and displays milestone (plan skip/unskip/reopen don't)
3. **Subjective dimension hints** – `_print_subjective_reset_hint()` suggests re-reviewing specific dimensions (plan doesn't)
4. **Next-command guidance** – `_print_next_command()` suggests what to do after resolve (plan doesn't)
5. **Rich query.json entry** – `_write_resolve_query_entry()` writes comprehensive context (plan skip writes nothing to query)
6. **Cluster expansion** – `_try_expand_cluster()` allows resolving entire clusters by name (plan reopen/skip don't have this)

**What plan commands DO that resolve doesn't:**
- `cmd_plan_skip()` supports permanent/temporary/false_positive modes with different logic paths
- `cmd_plan_describe()` and `cmd_plan_note()` add augmented descriptions/notes to findings
- `cmd_plan_unskip()` explicitly removes from skipped dict
- `cmd_plan_focus()` manages active cluster focus
- Plan commands use `resolve_ids_from_patterns()` helper which includes synthetic plan-only IDs
